A database-driven central online policy repository is planned.

What is the CSULB policy on on campus meetings and events?

<user>
Tell me about gift of public funds in the Policies and Protocols.
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policies and Protocols, section "Gift of Public Funds": California Constitution, Article 16, § 6 prohibits making gifts of any public funds. The state must receive commensurate value whenever its resources are used, including time, equipment, materials, supplies and facilities. Government Code § 8314 additionally prohibits the use of state resources for nonstate purposes, except uses that are "incidental and minimal" (e.g. de minimis personal telephone or photo copy use, see campus policy).
Source: https://www.csulb.edu/government-relations/policies-and-protocols
</bot>

I have a question about on and off campus political events at CSULB. What does the policy say?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policies and Protocols, section "On and Off Campus Political Events": Events that are political in nature - hosted by a political party or candidate for office - that do not have a legislative purpose, may not be hosted, co-sponsored, or sponsored by the campus, and must cover the full costs of the event activity on campus. No Public Funds may be expended to support a political event on campus as that would be considered a gift of public funds.

Summarize the Department Policies.
</user>
<bot>
According to the Department Policies: The California State University (CSU) police department policies are developed using Lexipol, a comprehensive policy management system based on constitutional law and nationally recognized best practices. As the industry standard used by most law enforcement agencies in California, Lexipol ensures our policies remain current, legally sound, and operationally effective.

I have a question about speaking before the senate at CSULB. What does the policy say?
</user>
<bot>
According to the ASI Bylaws 2025-26, section "Speaking Before the Senate": A quorum in the Senate will consist of a simple majority of all voting members. A simple majority is defined by 50% of the voting membership, plus one. Any member of the Associated Students will be assured the right to speak before the Senate.

The Executive Vice President will have other powers and perform such other duties as may be prescribed by law, by the articles of incorporation, or by these bylaws, or as may be prescribed by the Senate.

What does the CSULB ASI Bylaws 2025-26 say about rules and procedures?
</user>
<bot>
According to the ASI Bylaws 2025-26, section "Rules and Procedures": A quorum will be defined as a simple majority of voting members, and at least one non-voting member. A simple majority is defined by 50% of the voting membership, plus one. The Judiciary will provide for its own rules and procedures under the guidance of one or more of its non-voting members.

The Judiciary will: A. Interpret the provisions of the Bylaws or any other ASI document when a dispute arises.

I have a question about areas requiring senate approval at CSULB. What does the policy say?
</user>
<bot>
According to the ASI Bylaws 2025-26, section "Areas Requiring Senate Approval": With the exception of the annual operating budget, the Senate may by a 2/3 majority delegate to the Board authority for final approval of certain measures as specified by the Senate. All such delegations of authority must be reauthorized annually during the approval of the Senate Working Rules.

<user>
Can you explain the "Officers to Be Elected" part of the ASI Bylaws 2025-26?
</user>
<bot>
According to the ASI Bylaws 2025-26, section "Officers to Be Elected": There will be one general election in each academic year to be held in the spring semester. The following officers will be elected by the general student body: A. The President, Executive Vice President, Vice President of Finance, and six Senators-at-Large. B. Two Senators from each of the academic colleges.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/gov/Bylaws%202025-2026-v2.pdf
</bot>

<user>
Number of Votes Needed - what are the rules at CSULB?
</user>
<bot>
According to the ASI Bylaws 2025-26, section "Number of Votes Needed": A simple majority of all votes cast in the balloting procedure will be necessary to elect the President, Executive Vice President, and Vice President of Finance. Senators-at-Large will be elected by a plurality of all votes cast. Senators representing the academic colleges will be elected by a plurality of all votes cast for each office by the constituency of which the office is to represent.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/gov/Bylaws%202025-2026-v2.pdf
</bot>

<user>
Date of Assumption of Office - what are the rules at CSULB?
</user>
<bot>
According to the ASI Bylaws 2025-26, section "Date of Assumption of Office": Each elected officer will assume office and its duties on the first day of June. Any officer assuming a vacancy by election or succession will assume the office and its duties immediately upon fulfillment of all requirements for said office.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/gov/Bylaws%202025-2026-v2.pdf
</bot>

<user>
What does the CSULB ASI Bylaws 2025-26 say about length of term of office?
</user>
<bot>
According to the ASI Bylaws 2025-26, section "Length of Term of Office": Each elected officer will serve a term for a maximum of one year ending on May 31. The term of office for these officers is set by each Board, subject to the approval of the Senate. Officers may run for re-election an unlimited number of times with the exception of the President, Executive Vice President, and Vice President of Finance who will have a term limit of two years for serving in the same position.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/gov/Bylaws%202025-2026-v2.pdf
</bot>

<user>
What is the CSULB policy on initiative petition?
</user>
<bot>
According to the ASI Bylaws 2025-26, section "Initiative Petition": No student officer will hold more than one major elected or appointed office concurrently. No student may hold concurrently more than one office as defined in Chapter Two of these Bylaws. ARTICLE VIII INITIATIVE, REFERENDUM, AND RECALL Members of the Associated Students will have the power to initiate legislation by means of a petition signed by 5% of the regular membership.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/gov/Bylaws%202025-2026-v2.pdf
</bot>

<user>
Can you explain the "Referendum Petition" part of the ASI Bylaws 2025-26?
</user>
<bot>
According to the ASI Bylaws 2025-26, section "Referendum Petition": Any legislation passed by the Senate, except emergency or financial measures, may be subject to the referendum vote of the members of the Associated Students. Upon presentation to the President of a written petition signed by 5% of the regular members of the Associated Students, the Senate will repeal such legislation or submit it to a vote of the members of the Associated Students.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/gov/Bylaws%202025-2026-v2.pdf
</bot>

<user>
What does the CSULB ASI Bylaws 2025-26 say about initiative and referendum votes?
</user>
<bot>
According to the ASI Bylaws 2025-26, section "Initiative and Referendum Votes": The Senate may by 2⁄3 vote refer any proposed legislation to the members of the Associated Students for decision. Upon the presentation to the President of an initiative or referendum petition or notification of Senate referendum, the proposed legislation will be placed on the ballot of the next regular election for a vote of the Associated Students.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/gov/Bylaws%202025-2026-v2.pdf

In entering this agreement, CSU finds that certain functions important to its mission are more effectively accomplished by the use of an auxiliary organization rather than by the University under the usual state procedures.

What does the CSULB Policy on Complaint Management say about reporting complaints?

What is the CSULB policy on appeal of complaint resolution?
</user>

Give me an overview of the CSULB Policy on Conflicts of Interest.
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Conflicts of Interest: Associated Students, Incorporated (ASI) is a nonprofit, tax-exempt organization. Maintenance of its tax-exempt status is important both for its continued financial stability and for continuing public support. Therefore, the IRS as well as state regulatory and tax officials view the operations of ASI as a public trust, which is subject to scrutiny by and accountable to governmental authorities as well as to members of the public.

According to the Policy on Discrimation, Harrassment and Retaliation, section "Domestic Violence": Domestic violence is defined as abuse committed against someone who is a current or former spouse; current or former cohabitant; someone with whom the abuser has a child; someone with whom the abuser has or had a dating or engagement relationship; or a person similarly situated under California domestic or family violence law.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-discrimination-harassment-and-retaliation.pdf

</user>

According to the Policy on Discrimation, Harrassment and Retaliation, section "Stalking": Stalking is defined as engaging in a repeated Course of Conduct directed at a specific person that would cause a Reasonable Person to fear for their own or others' safety, or to suffer Substantial Emotional Distress.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-discrimination-harassment-and-retaliation.pdf

<user>
Can you explain the "Sexual Misconduct" part of the Policy on Discrimation, Harrassment and Retaliation?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Discrimation, Harrassment and Retaliation, section "Sexual Misconduct": All sexual activity between members of the ASI community must be based on Affirmative Consent. Engaging in any sexual activity without first obtaining Affirmative Consent to the specific activity constitutes Sexual Misconduct and is a violation of this policy, whether or not the conduct violates any civil or criminal law.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-discrimination-harassment-and-retaliation.pdf
</bot>

<user>
Can you explain the "Sexual Misconduct" part of the Policy on Discrimation, Harrassment and Retaliation?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Discrimation, Harrassment and Retaliation, section "Sexual Misconduct": A request for someone to use a condom or birth control does not, in and of itself, constitute Affirmative Consent. Affirmative Consent can be withdrawn or revoked. Consent to one form of sexual activity (or one sexual act) does not constitute consent to other forms of sexual activity. Consent given to sexual activity on one occasion does not constitute consent on another occasion.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-discrimination-harassment-and-retaliation.pdf
</bot>

<user>
What is the CSULB policy on sexual misconduct?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Discrimation, Harrassment and Retaliation, section "Sexual Misconduct": A person's own intoxication or incapacitation from drugs or alcohol does not diminish that person's responsibility to obtain Affirmative Consent before engaging in sexual activity. A person with a medical or mental disability may also lack the capacity to give consent. Sexual activity with a minor (a person under 18 years old) is not consensual, because a minor is considered incapable of giving consent due to age.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-discrimination-harassment-and-retaliation.pdf

</user>
<bot>

It may include an inquiry into the facts, but does not include an investigation. The Human Resources Office will attempt to resolve the Complainant's concern(s) promptly and effectively. The Human Resources Office will meet with the Complainant, the Respondent, and any other person(s) or witness(es) determined to be necessary for a resolution, to review the allegations and any responses.

Final Early Resolution - what are the rules at CSULB?
</user>
<bot>

The matter will be considered closed and the Complainant is precluded from filing a Formal Complaint concerning the same incident, except where the terms of the informal resolution have been violated or have been ineffective in stopping the alleged discrimination, harassment, or retaliation.

According to the Policy on Volunteers, section "Volunteer Recruitmen T and Selection": Volunteers will be recruited by ASI on a proactive basis, with the intent of broadening and expanding the volunteer involvement of the campus community. ASI shall recruit volunteers without regard to gender, disability, age, race, sexual orientation, or other protected status. A standard qualification for volunteer recruitment will be suitability to perform a task on behalf of the organization.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-volunteers.pdf
</bot>

Tell me about application in the Policy on Volunteers.
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Volunteers, section "Application": Persons interested in volunteering with ASI must complete a Volunteer Application and optional Confidential Information Statement. No person may be interviewed or otherwise considered for placement in a volunteer assignment unless a Volunteer Application has been completed and submitted to the Human Resources Office.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-volunteers.pdf
</bot>

<user>
Tell me about interviewing in the Policy on Volunteers.
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Volunteers, section "Interviewing": Before being assigned or appointed to a position, volunteers may be interviewed to ascertain their suitability for and interest in that position. Interviews are required in those cases where the volunteer is a non-student and/or the position has a high level of responsibility. The interview should determine the qualifications of the volunteer, their commitment to fulfill the requirements of the position, and should answer any questions that the volunteer might have about the position.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-volunteers.pdf

The orientation will inform the volunteers about ASI's history and mission and all the volunteer opportunities offered. The orientation should answer any questions that the volunteer might have about their position and their role as an ASI volunteer, including the provisions of this policy.

Background checks will be required for the following:; - - Any non-student volunteer who will have direct contact with CSULB students Any volunteer who will have direct contact with minors Any volunteer placed into a position that handles large amounts of cash Volunteers who do not agree to the background check may be refused assignment.

Volunteers who are under a course of treatment that might affect their volunteer work will not be accepted without written verification of suitability from their physician.

I have a question about volunteer placement at CSULB. What does the policy say?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Volunteers, section "Volunteer Placement": In placing a volunteer in a position, attention will be paid to the interests and capabilities of the volunteer and to the requirements of the volunteer position. No placement will be made unless the requirements of both the volunteer and the supervising staff can be met. No volunteer will ever be assigned to a "make-work" position and no position will be given to an unqualified or uninterested volunteer.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-volunteers.pdf

What is the purpose of the Policy on Whistleblower Protection?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Whistleblower Protection: Associated Students, Inc.'s (ASI) Policy on Business Ethics and Conduct and ASI's Policy on Fraud require officers, directors, employees, and volunteers to observe high standards of business and personal ethics in the conduct of their duties and responsibilities. It provides that employees and representatives of the organization must practice honesty and integrity in fulfilling their responsibilities and must comply with all applicable laws and regulations.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-whistleblower-protection.pdf
</bot>

I have a question about no retaliation at CSULB. What does the policy say?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Whistleblower Protection, section "No Retaliation": No stakeholder who, in good faith, reports a concern shall be subject to retaliation or, in the case of an employee, adverse employment consequences. Moreover, a volunteer or employee who retaliates against someone who has reported a concern in good faith is subject to discipline up to and including dismissal from the volunteer position or termination of employment.

Give me an overview of the CSULB Policy on Political Activities.
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Political Activities: Policy Statement It is the policy of the Associated Students, Incorporated that funds and money collected by or on behalf of the student body shall not be used to support or oppose any candidate for public office, whether partisan or not. Furthermore, funds shall not be used to support or oppose any issue before the voters of California or any subdivision thereof, or any city, municipality, or local governmental entity of any kind except as permitted by Section 89300 of the California Education Code.

What is the purpose of the Policy on Risk Management?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Risk Management: The activities of the Associated Students, Incorporated (ASI) are susceptible to varying degrees of risk whether it occurs on- or offcampus. To ensure that risks arising out of these activities are anticipated, managed, and mitigated, ASI has established a risk management program. The program is designed to protect the safety of all staff, students, and visitors as well as preserve ASI's physical property.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/Policy%20on%20Risk%20Mgmt_9.13.23.pdf

What is the CSULB Policy on Student Media about?

According to the Policy on Student Media: Purpose Associated Students, Incorporated (ASI) recognizes the educational and societal value of encouraging the uninhibited, robust, free and open discussion of issues and ideas on America's college and university campuses, as well as the legal protections afforded students' exercise of freedom of expression and press freedom, especially by the First Amendment to the United States Constitution.

<user>
Can you explain the "Obscene Material" part of the Policy on Student Media?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Student Media, section 2.1 (Obscene Material): Indecent or vulgar language is not obscene. 2.2 Libelous or Slanderous Material Libel and slander are not protected forms of expression. Libelous or slanderous statements are provably false and unprivileged statements of fact that do demonstrated injury to an individual's or business's reputation in the community.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-student-media.pdf
</bot>

<user>
What does the CSULB Policy on Student Media say about material that unlawfully invades a person's right to privacy?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Student Media, section 2.3 (Material that Unlawfully Invades a Person's Right to Privacy): Student media is protected by the First Amendment right to free speech, as long as the published or broadcast material does not violate personal privacy and is either verifiable as true or presented clearly as an opinion, not a statement of fact. It is often associated with a public figure's right to be left alone by the media, although many aspects of a public figure's life are not protected.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-student-media.pdf
</bot>

<user>
What does the CSULB Policy on Student Media say about material that unlawfully invades a person's right to privacy?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Student Media, section 2.3 (Material that Unlawfully Invades a Person's Right to Privacy): "False Light" is printing a private person's name or likeness in a way that is highly offensive and untrue, or portraying the individual in such as way that it would be highly offensive to a reasonable person. - "Appropriation of Name, Likeness or Identity" is the appropriation of a private person's name, likeness or identity by the media for commercial gain.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-student-media.pdf
</bot>

<user>
What does "False Light" mean in the Policy on Student Media?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Student Media, section 2.3 (Material that Unlawfully Invades a Person's Right to Privacy): False Light: printing a private person's name or likeness in a way that is highly offensive and untrue, or portraying the individual in such as way that it would be highly offensive to a reasonable person.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-student-media.pdf
</bot>

<user>
Tell me about material that unlawfully invades a person's right to privacy in the Policy on Student Media.
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Student Media, section 2.3 (Material that Unlawfully Invades a Person's Right to Privacy): 2.4 Material that Will Cause "A Material and Substantial Disruption of Campus Activities" Disruption includes student rioting, unlawful seizures of property, destruction of property, or substantial student participation in a campus boycott, sit-in, walk-out, or other related form of activity.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-student-media.pdf
</bot>

<user>
Tell me about material that unlawfully invades a person's right to privacy in the Policy on Student Media.
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Student Media, section 2.3 (Material that Unlawfully Invades a Person's Right to Privacy): Mere undifferentiated fear or apprehension of disturbance is not enough; ASI must be able to show affirmatively substantial facts that reasonably support a forecast of likely disruption. In determining whether student media is disruptive, consideration must be given to the context of the distribution as well as the content of the material.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-student-media.pdf
</bot>

<user>
What does the CSULB Policy on Student Media say about material that unlawfully invades a person's right to privacy?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Student Media, section 2.3 (Material that Unlawfully Invades a Person's Right to Privacy): The services of the attorney for local news media or the free legal services of the Student Press Law Center (www.splc.org) are recommended. Any legal fees charged in connection with the consultation will be paid from the operating budget of the respective media outlet. The final decision of whether the material is to be published or broadcast will be left to the student editor or station manager, respectively.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-student-media.pdf
</bot>

<user>
What does the CSULB Policy on Student Media say about material that unlawfully invades a person's right to privacy?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Student Media, section 2.3 (Material that Unlawfully Invades a Person's Right to Privacy): Ban the publication or broadcast by students of material written by non-students; Prohibit the endorsement of candidates for student office or for public office at any level; or Engage in any activity or cause to be done to student media anything where the effect is to control, diminish, manipulate or otherwise censor student media or to dismiss, punish or retaliate against student media staff where such action is motivated by the otherwise lawful content or newsgathering activities of student media.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-student-media.pdf
</bot>

<user>
Explain the CSULB Policy on Hazard Communication Program in short.
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Hazard Communication Program: Hazard Communication Policy Associated Students, Incorporated (ASI) shall implement a continuing, systematic program to communicate, evaluate, control and record the degree of employee exposure to hazardous chemicals and physical agents both regulated and non-regulated. Physical agents can take on different physical forms such as liquids, solids, gases, vapors, fumes and mists whether they are contained or not.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-hazard-communication-program.pdf
</bot>

</user>

According to the Policy on Hazard Communication Program, section "Reading Labels and Material/safety Data Sheets (m/sds)": Labels A product label on both the original and secondary containers must be reviewed prior to working with the material. Each label will have two important pieces of information:; - Hazardous Substances (Identity) Hazard Warnings The label on the original container will also state the name and address of the manufacturer.

I have a question about physical and health hazard of the hazardous substances used at CSULB. What does the policy say?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Hazard Communication Program, section "Physical and Health Hazard of the Hazardous Substances Used": Employees will be trained about the hazards of the substances in their work areas. We will use the category approach, and each employee will be made aware that information is available on the specific hazards of individual hazardous substances through M/SDS.

A. WHAT ARE HAZARDOUS SUBSTANCES? Hazardous substances are chemicals which have been identified as requiring special precautions during their use due to their toxic effects, physical properties like flammability, explositivity and reactivity or potential to adversely affect the environment,.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-hazard-communication-program.pdf

<user>
I have a question about hazardous substances training guide at CSULB. What does the policy say?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Hazard Communication Program, section "Hazardous Substances Training Guide": Medical experts have reviewed the data on many hazardous substances to determine how toxic they are and to set permissible exposure levels (OSHA standards) called PELs to limit the dose that workers receive of these substances. These standards are included on M/SDS and are used to evaluate workplace exposure monitoring results.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-hazard-communication-program.pdf
</bot>

<user>
What is the CSULB policy on hazardous substances training guide?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Hazard Communication Program, section "Hazardous Substances Training Guide": WHAT ARE THE TYPES OF TOXIC EFFECTS? In general, there are two major types of toxic effects: acute and chronic. ACUTE Acute effects are observed when the exposure is sufficiently large to have an immediate and usually shortterm effect. Examples of acute toxicity are chemical skin burns, asphyxiation, and sudden poisonings.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-hazard-communication-program.pdf
</bot>

<user>
What does the CSULB Policy on Hazard Communication Program say about engineering controls?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Hazard Communication Program, section "Engineering Controls": Ventilation systems and physical isolation of the chemical from the worker are examples of engineering controls that are the surest means of controlling exposures to hazardous substances. PERSONAL PROTECTIVE EQUIPMENT (PPE) Respirators and impervious gloves are examples of personal protective equipment that reduce a worker's personal exposure to hazardous substances, but that do not improve the overall workplace environment.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-hazard-communication-program.pdf
</bot>

<user>
Tell me about engineering controls in the Policy on Hazard Communication Program.
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Hazard Communication Program, section "Engineering Controls": Personal hygiene is particularly important when handling highly toxic substances. OSHA regulations for certain substances prescribe personal hygiene practices. FOLLOWING LABEL WARNINGS Labeling, as required by the Hazard Communication Regulation, is an important control measure, if workers read, understand, and follow label instructions.

<user>
What does the CSULB Policy on Injury and Illness Prevention Plan say about correction of unsafe conditions?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Injury and Illness Prevention Plan, section "Correction of Unsafe Conditions": Serious Hazards Serious hazards pose an immediate threat to employee safety or health. When a serious hazard is identified, the work will be immediately stopped and employees will inform a supervisor. If the supervisor deems the condition an imminent hazard, the hazard will be immediately corrected or the job stopped and employees removed from harm until corrective measures are taken.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-injury-and-illness-prevention-plan.pdf
</bot>

<user>
Tell me about correction of unsafe conditions in the Policy on Injury and Illness Prevention Plan.
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Injury and Illness Prevention Plan, section "Correction of Unsafe Conditions": Non-Serious Hazards Non-serious hazards do not pose an immediate threat to employee safety or health, but could pose a risk through repeated employee exposure or equipment failure during an emergency. Examples might include a blocked electrical panel, a broken electrical outlet face plate, or nonfunctioning eye-wash station.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-injury-and-illness-prevention-plan.pdf
</bot>

<user>
Correction of Unsafe Conditions - what are the rules at CSULB?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Injury and Illness Prevention Plan, section "Correction of Unsafe Conditions": Action Plans When a corrective action for an unsafe condition or work practice cannot be completed immediately or will involve multiple steps, the supervisor, or other designated individual, will develop an action plan for its abatement. The action plan will include the name of the individual responsible for correcting the hazard and an estimated completion date.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-injury-and-illness-prevention-plan.pdf

What is the CSULB policy on accident investigation?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Injury and Illness Prevention Plan, section "Accident Investigation": Purpose An accident or "near miss" is a signal that there is a problem in the workplace. The primary goal of the accident investigation program is to prevent future incidents by identifying the root cause of the accident and using that knowledge to correct the problem. Additionally, the information is used to prepare reports required by Federal and State law, and the Workers' Compensation Insurance carrier.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-injury-and-illness-prevention-plan.pdf

The Employee Training and Safety Coordinator or designee must then investigate the circumstances of the accident and determine the root-cause.

The individual designated on the current Signature Authorization Card and/or Delegation of Authority form must authorize each transaction and entry in writing.

Copies of invoices and supporting documents will be filed, maintained and secured by the ASI Business Office Administrative.

Give me an overview of the CSULB Policy on Business Property.
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Business Property: The Associated Students, Incorporated (ASI) Policy on Business Property is designed to ensure proper accountability for ASI property. The regulations contained herein are intended to provide accurate records for the acquisition, control, and disposal of property. The combination of accurate accounting records and strong internal controls are designed to protect against lost and to detect unauthorized use of ASI property.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-business-property.pdf

I have a question about capitalization at CSULB. What does the policy say?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Business Property, section 1.0 (Capitalization): 1.0 ASI property shall be capitalized for accounting purposes when it represents an investment of $10,000 or more per unit and has an estimate useful life of at least three years. Capitalization means the asset is recorded in the accounting records as a fixed asset and accountability is maintained until disposal of the asset occurs.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-business-property.pdf

What is the CSULB policy on definitions?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Cash Handling and Management, section "Definitions": For purposes of this policy, the terms used are defined as follows: Term Definition Cash Payment for goods or services or a gift or contribution to ASI that is received in any one of the following forms: currency, coin, check, money order, traveler's check, electronic funds transfer, credit card transaction, or debit card transaction Cash collection points Departments or other units that receive and handle cash on a regular basis Deposit The deposit of cash with the A.S.

<user>
Can you explain the "Conditions for Employment in Cash Handling Environment" part of the Policy on Cash Handling and Management?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Cash Handling and Management, section "Conditions for Employment in Cash Handling Environment": This policy applies to all ASI personnel who handle cash during the processing of any transaction, or who retain, store, and/or safeguard cash prior to its deposit with the A.S. Business Office. 1.0 Individuals who are hired into full-time positions that handle large amounts of cash may be have an appropriate background check conducted prior to employment.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/Policy%20on%20Cash%20Handling%20and%20Management%20060426.pdf
</bot>

<user>
I have a question about cash collections at CSULB. What does the policy say?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Cash Handling and Management, section "Cash Collections": All cash must be collected at the A.S. Business Office front counter by a Customer Service Representative except as noted below under "Cash Collection Points." In the absence of a Customer Service Representative, an A.S. Business Office Staff member shall be assigned to the front desk to perform this duty.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/Policy%20on%20Cash%20Handling%20and%20Management%20060426.pdf
</bot>

<user>
Cash Collections - what are the rules at CSULB?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Cash Handling and Management, section "Cash Collections": The check should still be restrictively endorsed immediately upon receipt, logged as an unidentified receipt if needed, and held in a secure location pending clarification. The sequence of pre-numbered cash receipts must be accounted for and follow-up done on any missing documents. Stacks of new receipts to be placed in use must be accounted for to ensure that the numerical sequence with the prior stack is maintained.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/Policy%20on%20Cash%20Handling%20and%20Management%20060426.pdf
</bot>

<user>
What does the CSULB Policy on Cash Handling and Management say about unidentified cash?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Cash Handling and Management, section "Unidentified Cash": Mail will be opened by the Customer Service Representative(s), who will prepare a detailed listing of cash and checks received each day. This listing must include the name, amount, invoice number (if applicable), and date.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/Policy%20on%20Cash%20Handling%20and%20Management%20060426.pdf
